Per-service debt + go-lives: paperless, inboxclean, miniflux, mail-relay, immich, forgejo, hermes, bank-sync, browser-history, dnsblockd, discordsync, searxng, CV, attic, signal, monitor365, google-sync, tq pool units, PMA units. (twenty + all Docker-era services removed 2026-10-08.)

Domain LIBRARY of the [TODO system](../../TODO_LIST.md) — every open item for this domain, any lifecycle. The dispatch QUEUE of agent-actionable (`[ready]`) items is `TODO_LIST.md`; the tq pool harvests only the queue.

House rules (enforced by convention, see AGENTS.md → "TODO System"): file an item under the domain that owns the FIX, not the symptom it mentions; one item = one ask + `**Source:**` pointer — verification narratives go to the status report, never the item; `[x]` rows are pruned to `CHANGELOG.md` at every pass; NO system-state narratives here (AGENTS.md and `docs/services/*` runbooks own state).

Tag legend: `[ready]` agent-actionable · `[blocked:user]` needs sudo/browser/external console/owner hands · `[blocked:push]` needs an upstream push/tag (agents implement, never push) · `[blocked:deploy]` waits on a deploy · `[watch]` time-gated verification · `[decision]` owner question.

- [x] [decision] **Bidirectional contacts + calendar: D1 + D2 — DECIDED 2026-10-29 (owner, in conversation): D1 = Mesh B** (Stalwart owns contacts + calendar; Google = bi-directional edges, one writer per leg; Kith demotes to relations + read-only people projection); **D2 = field-scoped writes + deletion quarantine + per-run caps + queue-not-push for attendee events.** Constitution amended (`crm/docs/DATA-MESH.md`); execution = `docs/planning/2026-10-29_22-11_stalwart-single-truth-pareto-execution-plan.md`. Original ask (historical): supersedes the narrower "who owns the calendar?" ask below it. Nothing owns either entity type today: Google holds the only real bytes; the CRM journal is the ratified contacts truth but holds zero people contacts; calendar has no owner at all (no CalDAV server — the mail stack advertises SMTP/IMAP/JMAP/CardDAV only — and no DAV client: khal unused, DMS events off, no vdirsyncer/Radicale/Baikal). D1 = Mesh A (Kith keeps contacts, Stalwart owns calendar) vs Mesh B (Stalwart owns both, Kith demotes to relations); D2 = amend the CRM mesh rule "NEVER writes back" to field-scoped low-trust writes + a conflict queue, or drop the bidirectional ask. Three ownership doctrines currently disagree (SystemNix docs/agents/data-ownership.md; crm/docs/DATA-MESH.md ratified 2026-10-19; pbx-artmann/docs/architecture/data-ownership.md) — the same 2026-10-19 decision is cited by both sides for opposite conclusions, so D1 must retire one. Secondary decisions in the same document: D3 (which account stays symmetric), D4 (contacts first vs calendar too), D5 (dedup policy for the overlap + the 139 staged Twenty contacts), D6 (adapter placement + cadence). Verifications #1/#2 in the proposal ride the nix-email CalDAV probe row and pbx-artmann's `pim-ownership-declare` row, not this one. **Source:** docs/planning/2026-10-29_21-18_contacts-calendar-mesh-proposal.html §12

- [ ] [ready] **Name the backend that compresses media responses** — the 2026-10-06 Caddy encode investigation proved Caddy's innocence (default Content-Type allowlist live-verified on the system 2.11.4 binary: html/json/svg+xml → zstd; png/jpeg/webp/mp4/octet-stream → NOT encoded; runbook section "encode / compression behavior" in docs/services/caddy.md) and identified the mechanism: `reverse_proxy` forwards the client's `Accept-Encoding` unchanged, browsers attach it to every fetch, so backends with compression middleware (NestJS/Express `compression`, Django GZipMiddleware, …) compress media themselves. - [x] [ready] **CA parity has no guard: add the fingerprint compare to post-deploy-check.sh** — parity was verified byte-identical 2026-10-28 and NOTHING enforced it. **DONE 2026-10-28 (plan T02):** `post-deploy-check.sh §22` now runs on every deploy via `scripts/lib/ca-parity-smoke.sh` (sourced lib, fixture-tested by `scripts/test-ca-parity-smoke.sh` — 8 legs with REAL openssl certs — and the `checks.dnsblockd-ca-parity-selftest` flake check). It enumerates every cert in `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` (296 today) and compares each `dnsblockd-CA` fingerprint to `/run/secrets/dnsblockd_ca_cert`: absent from the bundle → FAIL, a DIFFERENT dnsblockd-CA also trusted → FAIL (retired key keeps trust), identical-but-duplicated → WARN, <90d to expiry → WARN. Live verdict 2026-10-28: **WARN (duplicate)** — see the new row below. Rotation procedure written up in `docs/services/dnsblockd.md` 'CA rotation'. **Source:** same review §E9 (issue #7)
- [ ] [ready] **Find the SECOND writer of the dnsblockd CA in the system trust bundle** — `/etc/ssl/certs/ca-certificates.crt` carries the `dnsblockd-CA` PEM **twice** with IDENTICAL fingerprint `EE:A9:E1:…:49:88` (positions 278/279 of 296; benign for trust, so this is hygiene, not a security hole). `security.pki.certificates` has exactly ONE entry at eval (`nix eval … config.security.pki.certificates | length` → 1) and only ONE source file in the repo (`platforms/nixos/system/dns-blocker-config.nix:244`, `rg -l` on the PEM signature), so a second mechanism appends it — find it before the next rotation, because a half-understood trust path is exactly how the §22 parity guard ends up trusting the wrong copy. **Source:** plan T02 — the §22 guard's live WARN verdict 2026-10-28
- [x] [ready] **Blocklist freshness telemetry: the blocking data is eval-pinned with no age signal** — **DONE 2026-10-28 (plan T14/T15):** `dnsblockd-otel-observer` (oneshot + 60s timer) emits `dnsblockd_blocklist_age_seconds` and `dnsblockd_blocklist_refresh_stale` into the node_exporter textfile dir, alarmed by Gatus "DNS Blocker Blocklist Freshness" (5m, 30-day threshold) via the service's own registry entry. NOTE the measurement had to change from the plan: Nix store files carry mtime = 1, so "max mtime of the loaded lists" was meaningless (it read ~56 years). The observer instead hashes the loaded set and remembers the first epoch it saw that hash — i.e. seconds since the set last changed = seconds since anyone refreshed. Verified against the shipped script text: fresh set → age 0/stale 0; set backdated 40d → stale 1; empty set (tempAllowAll) → age -1/stale 0. Refresh cadence stays MANUAL by design (the script edits SRI hashes that need a commit + deploy); the metric is the alarm. **Source:** same review (issue #8)

- [ ] [blocked:user] **DiscordSync Oct 2–14 01:01 data-loss recovery — owner picks the path** (root cause FOUND 2026-10-15: NOT corruption — the 01:01 heal misread a transient `database is locked` as corruption, `.recover` failed on the same lock, and the WAL-BLIND snapshot restore from `@.20261013T2314` replaced the live 1.1 GB DB with the QLC shadow's days-stale main file — discarding ~29k messages — then `rm -f` destroyed the live `-wal`; evidence: heal journal 01:01:52–01:03:47, live-DB day-counts stop at Oct 1, 29,476 `discord.message.created` dispatched Oct 7–12 per journal, the 1.1 GB quarantine ≈ the full pre-incident DB). Path A (simplest, complete for what Discord still serves): per-channel `POST /api/backfill/restart?channel_id=` — the dashboard's Reset button, needs the API key — with backfillOnStartup temporarily enabled so the bot re-fetches history (idempotent; rate-limited; heavy on media). Path B (surgical; also recovers messages since-DELETED from Discord): stop service → dump live (preserves Oct 14 13:32→now) → integrity_check the quarantine `discordsync.db.corrupt-20261014T010152` → swap it in → backfill only the Oct 14 01:00→now tail. Both need owner sudo/API key; ~5.3 GB quarantine graveyard (4.2 GB Sep 20 + 1.1 GB Oct 14) stays owner-gated until AFTER recovery. **API-key prerequisite RESOLVED (2026-10-15 code-read): NONE** — the auth middleware only installs when `APIKey` is configured (`internal/api/server.go` wraps the handler `if apiKey != ""`), the 2026-09-02 "no auth key configured" note still holds (unauthenticated API reads work), and the listener is loopback-bound — Path A's `POST /api/backfill/restart` works as-is, no key setup step. Residual open question (small): whether Aug 4 / Sep 20 "corruptions" were the same lock-misread class.

**RE-ADJUDICATED (2026-10-15, this session):** (1) **Aug 4 = GENUINE corruption, NOT lock-misread** — the Aug-03/04 wdt-crash reports verify the DB was the ONLY corrupt DB among all readable DBs after the hard crash, and the BTRFS snapshots were hours-fresh pre-hot-migration (restore source legitimate); the follow-on readonly crash-loop was the missing chown-back (different defect, since fixed in-script). (2) **Sep 20 = cascade RAN, trigger UNKNOWABLE** — the graveyard's 4.2 GB Sep 20 quarantine proves the recovery cascade executed (integrity failed or was misread — indistinguishable; journald retention only reaches Oct 6 and no surviving report captured the heal's stderr; the 4.2 GB size fits the S5 DB-bloat era). (3) **Oct 14 = lock-misread, fully proven.** Recurrence story: 1 confirmed + 1 possible + 1 absent — and the deployed controls (lock-guard, hot-tier snapshot refusal, WAL-pair, chown-back) cover BOTH classes regardless. **Source:** 2026-10-15 diagnosis session — dump forensics + heal journal + script read (this row replaces the closed root-cause half of the old 461)

- [ ] [watch] **ADS-1800W USB bus drop-off class (no recovery in-process or in-session)** — the device disappeared mid-scan at 09:32 (kernel released the blocked URB only at 10:01:36 — `usb 6-1: USB disconnect, device number 11`), `timeout` returned rc=124, and the scanner stayed gone until a **physical replug** at 10:50:42 (device number 13). Neither the wrapper nor an agent session can reset the port (rebind/authorized toggle are root-owned, `usbreset` needs root). If it recurs during the campaign, the candidate fix is a root-side recovery unit; the wrapper's rc=124 branch already probes `scanimage -L` and tells the operator to replug. **Source:** this session — `journalctl -k` usb 6-1 events + `pgrep`/`scanimage -L` probes

- [ ] [watch] **ADS-1800W driver-level failure burst: rc=139 SIGSEGV + rc=1 device-open + rc=6 jam (all self-recovered)** — the 2026-10-19 end-of-day sweep of `watch.log` shows the flaky classes beyond rc=4: **332 × rc=4** (seating), **2 × rc=6** (`Batch terminated, 0 pages scanned` — SANE JAMMED), **5 × rc=1** (`scanimage: open of device brother5:bus6;dev1 failed: Invalid argument` — the device could not even be opened), and **1 × rc=139** at 12:15:06 (`timeout: the monitored command dumped core` — scanimage **segfaulted**; no core landed in the repo, and systemd-coredump holds only quickshell cores from 2026-10-18). The burst ran 12:15:06 → 12:15:44 and then cleared by itself: the next polls returned rc=7 (empty ADF) with no replug. So there are at least three distinct device-level failure modes behind "the scanner is being flaky", all of which the WARN text currently explains as if they were rc=4 seating problems. If a burst does NOT self-clear, the recovery is a replug (see the row above); the counting belongs in the runbook so a rising rate is visible rather than absorbed by retries. **Source:** `~/.cache/scan-to-paperless/watch.log`, swept 2026-10-19 23:2x (own session)

- [ ] [decision] **Arm dnsblockd pprof (`services.dns-blocker.pprofEnabled`) for wedge forensics (OWNER)** — landed OFF in plan T06 with a render-test variant. Upstream mounts `/debug/pprof/*` on the LOOPBACK stats listener (`stats_addr = 127.0.0.1`) and gates every route behind the dashboard bearer token (`registerPprofHandlers`, handlers.go:2716-2727 at lock `f9bb6477`), so arming it exposes nothing to the LAN. Why it matters: the ONLY current channel for the 2026-08-27/2026-09-06 stats-API wedge class is `scripts/dnsblockd-goroutine-dump.sh`, which reads stacks by sending SIGQUIT — i.e. by KILLING the process, which is exactly why the 08-27 root cause was never found. With pprof armed, `go tool pprof http://127.0.0.1:<statsPort>/debug/pprof/goroutine?debug=2` captures the same stacks from a RUNNING instance. Cost of arming: one authenticated debug endpoint on a loopback port. Cost of not arming: one restart per wedge diagnosis. **Source:** plan T06 Appendix A.4 (2026-10-28)
